**PHISH-01 — Phishing triage run (`phish-3`)** · Status: **Review** · Contributor: **Boey** · Branch: `phish-01-triage-run` (cut from `origin/main` at `bc545d7`, 28 October 2026, upstream unset) · Plan approved by Boey on 28 October 2026. Boey took over Athithya's phishing follow-ups (owner agreement quoted in the master handover; separate branch; tell Athithya when done).

- **Concept:** the 20-email Patient Zero investigation is replaced by a run of single-email triage rounds. Each round the player presses **Report** or **Trust**; after Report they tap the suspicious part (sender, reply-to, link, attachment/QR, request line).
- **Modes (from attempt context, no shared-contract change):** class context = timed ranked run, mode `standard`, one 180 s `deadlineAt`, ends at time-up or the 3rd mistake, capped at 60 rounds; personal context = untimed practice, mode `practice`, 12 rounds, no mistake limit, explanation after every round. The browser calls complete at time-up; resuming an expired run completes and saves it.
- **Difficulty:** fixed by round: rounds 1-4 tier 1, 5-9 tier 2, 10+ tier 3; about 60% genuine emails throughout (generator `triage-2`, see Revision). Content: lookalike/display-name spoof/reply-to mismatch/link text vs destination (ported from the g-phish-03 generator tricks and pools), link-less BEC, inert QR and attachment emails, legitimate urgent-looking decoys. Synthetic `.example` domains only; no clickable links.
- **Scoring (server only):** correct call 10/20/30 by tier, +10 for the right part; streak multiplier x1 (streak 0-2), x2 (3-5), x3 (6+). Trusting a phish or reporting a legitimate email is a mistake and resets the streak; reporting a phish with the wrong part scores the call without the +10 and is not a mistake.
- **Server:** `generator/` (seeded, round email from seed + round index; seed and answers never leave the server; opaque round IDs); ruleset `phish-3` (`competitive: true`, one action per round `{ call, partId? }`, `canComplete` at time-up, 3rd mistake or 12th practice round); `phish-3` current in `attempts/production-registry.js`, `phish-2` stays registered non-current so old results and unfinished runs still work. Public state: current round email only, previous round outcome and explanation, score, streak, mistakes, deadline.
- **Client:** email card with tappable parts, Report/Trust, timer/score/streak/mistakes bar with screen-reader time warnings, per-round feedback, debrief; `useTriageRun` composable; How to play rewritten; old `phish-2` results shown as a score summary; inbox/investigation components removed. Styling stays under `.phish-game`; 375 px full-width card, 44 px controls, Tab through parts, reduced motion.
- **Collision check (28 October 2026):** no other active claim on the phishing module; `origin/g-phish-03-generated-cases` and `origin/g-phish-02-continuation` are unmerged and superseded (Athithya agreed a fresh adapter replaces them, SAVE-01 record); only g-phish-03's `generator/` random/pools/tricks code is ported. `origin/g-phish-post-mortem` is merged.
- **Course basis (slide text read 28 October 2026, file-order slide numbers):** the CS440 decks mention phishing only in passing. `1-basics.pptx` slide 25 (threat sources) lists "Phishers" as individuals or small groups who run phishing schemes to steal identities or information, and bot-nets that distribute phishing schemes. `12a-web-security-partII.pptx` slide 6 (CSRF attack pattern) has the user clicking a link in an email. `06-authentication v2.pptx` slide 19 (password search space) does not mention phishing; it says passwords can be guessed via social engineering. No deck teaches email triage, so the learning objective is a team design. Slide images were not viewed; only the slide text.
- **Planned checks:** server unit (generator determinism and content safety, tier ramp, scoring/streak, end conditions, no answers before a call), client mount tests, integration (class timed vs personal practice, deadline, ranking, other players' runs hidden), one timed and one practice E2E (forced 2D).
- **Docs:** `client/src/games/phishing-post-mortem/README.md` (Presentation summary and sections), `server/src/modules/games/phishing-post-mortem/README.md` (phish-3 contract, generator section, phish-2 legacy), `README.md` ("Using the app" row and an update note on the G-PHISH blueprint), `client/src/features/leaderboard/README.md`, `server/src/modules/leaderboards/README.md`, `docs/saved-game-integration.md`, `DESIGN.md` (phishing colour, fitted-layout and town-table lines), and this file (this record, a note on the G-PHISH record and blueprint, and the stale "uses no database" phishing claim in the data-layer summary). `agents.md` not edited (see Next action).
- **Out of scope, noted:** `client/src/features/auth/__tests__/guards.test.js:139` posts to a stale `/verdict` path.
- **Verified (28 October 2026):** server unit `triage-generator.test.js` and `triage-ruleset.test.js`; client `triage-run.test.js`, `triage-components.test.js`, `triage-game.test.js` and `howToPlay.test.js`; `server/tests/integration/phishing-post-mortem/phishing.test.js` 10/10; `tests/e2e/phishing-post-mortem.spec.js` 20/20 across the desktop and mobile projects; leaderboards integration 12/12; page-fit check for Phishing 1 passed, 1 skipped. Full suites on the branch: `pnpm test:unit` exit 0, 124 files, 1419/1419; `node scripts/run-with-test-db.mjs integration --no-file-parallelism` 146 passed, 63 skipped, only `admin/lifecycle-races.test.js` (7) failing on its replica-set guard, which passes 7/7 with `--replica-set` (exit 0); plain `pnpm test:integration` with file parallelism fails 38 in sign-in/setup helpers across 14 files (load, as in earlier records); `pnpm build` exit 0; `pnpm lint` errors only in `.local-handover/` plus 6 pre-existing ADMIN-04 files (`server/src/modules/admin/service.js:328`, `delete-account.test.js`, `workspace.test.js:233`), none in this change.
- **Not verified:** manual browser pass on desktop and at 375 px; the real-time 3:00 expiry watched by eye; screen-reader announcements (timer warnings and round outcomes) with an actual screen reader; revised look and motion, timed stamp badge wrap at 375 px.

**ADMIN-05 — Reissue or cancel pending administrators** · Status: **Review** · Contributor: **Boey** · Branch: `admin-05-admin-reissue` (cut from `origin/main` at `51dda6f`, 28 October 2026, upstream unset) · Approved by Boey on 28 October 2026 (defaults plus a Cancel button). Closes the ADMIN-03 limit that a lost administrator link needed a new account.

- **Behaviour:** administrators with `admin:instructors:manage` see a **Pending administrators** list in the Instructors tab (username, email, link expiry or "Link expired") with **Reissue link** and **Cancel**. Pending means roles exactly `['admin']`, `accountStatus: 'disabled'` and no consumed activation token (administrators cannot be suspended, so this is never-activated), checked inside the transaction. Reissue revokes the old link through the existing `issueActivation` and shows the new one once in `ActivationLinkDialog`. Cancel (confirmed in the browser) deletes the account, its tokens and sessions and audits `admin.administrator.cancel` in one transaction. A target that is no longer pending returns 404 ("This administrator is no longer pending"). One reissue or cancel runs at a time so a second reissue cannot revoke the link being shown. The instructor reissue route is unchanged and still rejects administrators; it still re-enables a suspended instructor, which `server/src/modules/admin/README.md` documents.
- **API:** `GET /api/admin/administrators/pending` → `{ administrators: [{ id, username, email, provisionedAt, linkExpiresAt, linkStatus }], page, total }`; `POST /api/admin/administrators/:userId/activation` → `{ activationUrl, expiresAt }`; `POST /api/admin/administrators/:userId/delete` → `{ outcome: 'cancelled' }`. POSTs use the session, activation-write limit and CSRF/origin guards; bodies and queries must be empty.
- **Change:** `server/src/modules/admin/{service.js,router.js,README.md}`, `server/src/modules/audit/{policy.js,README.md}`, `server/tests/integration/admin/workspace.test.js`, `server/tests/unit/audit/policy.test.js`, `client/src/features/admin/{api.js,pages/AdminPage.vue,components/PendingAdministratorsPanel.vue (new),components/AuditEventsPanel.vue,README.md}`, `client/src/features/admin/__tests__/{admin.test.js,api.test.js}`, `tests/e2e/admin-workspace.spec.js`.
- **Collision check:** no admin reissue or cancel existed (rg for reissue/cancel in admin, recovery and tests); ADMIN-03 is Boey's.
- **Docs:** agents.md §5 (one clause); README "Using the app"; admin server and client READMEs; audit README; this record.
- **Verification (28 Oct 2026):** `server/tests/integration/admin/workspace.test.js` with `--replica-set` 9/9 in 7 of 8 runs (one early run failed "reissues a link that replaces the old one" with the error not captured; 3 targeted reruns clean); `server/tests/unit/audit/policy.test.js` 37/37; `client/src/features/admin` vitest 57/57; `tests/e2e/admin-workspace.spec.js` 8 passed exit 0; `pnpm build` exit 0; eslint clean apart from the existing `service.js:328` `no-useless-assignment` error already on main; prettier clean on touched files except `service.js`, which already failed on main.
- **Next action:** Boey decides on merge; watch the reissue integration test for a repeat of the uncaptured failure.

**BOARD-01 — Class leaderboards** · Status: **Review** · Contributor: **Boey** · Branch: `board-01-class-leaderboards` (cut from `origin/main` at `9e6c3a1`, 27 October 2026, upstream unset). Plan approved by Boey on 27 October 2026. Spec: [BOARD-01](#board-01--cohort-leaderboards).

- **Collision check (27 October 2026):** no leaderboard code on `main`, in local or remote branch names, or in the `admin-01-b-admin-controls` (Codex admin-controls) worktree. Existing references are only the `/leaderboard` ComingSoon placeholder (`client/src/router/index.js`), nav links (`Navbar.vue:34`, `PortalLayout.vue:30`), the pixel-wipe label, docs mentions, and an empty `server/src/modules/leaderboards/.gitkeep`. Remote branch contents, PRs and teammates' machines were not inspected.
- **Dependencies:** COH-01 and GAME-01-A (attempt foundation, in Review) on `main`; the ranking-rule change on `main` (`9e6c3a1`).
- **Decisions (Boey, 27 October 2026):** per-game boards with Best score and All-time tabs. Best: each student's best score, competition ranks (1, 1, 3), ties ordered by earliest completion reaching that score. All-time: total points (`round(100 · score / maxScore)` per completed attempt) with plays and best, ties ordered by username. Same game and ruleset only; default ruleset is the latest used in the class. Competitive rulesets rank only `competitiveEligible` attempts on the Best tab; other rulesets count all completed class-context attempts, labelled "Practice best scores". All-time counts every completed class run. Eligibility is rechecked at read time (current active membership, pure student). Personal context is excluded. The signed-in user's own row is highlighted with bold username and a screen-reader "(you)", plus a "Your rank" line when off-page. Page size 25. Access via `cohort:play` (active members, assigned instructors, admins) with a uniform 404. `agents.md` §5 is amended to allow plays/total/best on the all-time tab. Saving adapters for CLI, Phishing, Social and Clearance are separate tasks, each needing its owner's recorded agreement (team permission reported by Boey on 27 October 2026); they are not part of BOARD-01.
- **Scope:** new `server/src/modules/leaderboards/` (`rank.js`, `policy.js`, `service.js`, `router.js`, README); additive mount in `server/src/app.js`; new `client/src/features/leaderboard/` (`LeaderboardPage.vue`, `LeaderboardFilters.vue`, `LeaderboardTable.vue`, `api.js`, `leaderboard.css`, README); additive `/leaderboard` route in `client/src/router/index.js`. Tests in `server/tests/unit/leaderboards`, `server/tests/integration/leaderboards`, the client feature `__tests__`, and `tests/e2e/leaderboard.spec.js`. Docs: `agents.md` §5, TASKS.md, README "Using the app", module READMEs, attempts README.
- **API:** `GET /api/cohorts/:cohortId/leaderboard/games`; `GET /api/cohorts/:cohortId/leaderboard?gameId=&rulesetVersion=&view=best|all-time&page=`.
- **Verification (27 October 2026):** leaderboard server unit 28/28; leaderboard integration 12/12 (foreign/malformed/pending/removed access, removed-after-play, staff/dual-role/personal excluded, rulesets not mixed, competitive filtering, instructor/admin access, exact row keys); client mount tests 10/10; `tests/e2e/leaderboard.spec.js` 4/4 twice (desktop and mobile). `pnpm test:unit` 1200/1200 with the known 2 phishing `scrollTo` rejections (exit 1); `pnpm lint` errors only under `.local-handover/`; `pnpm build` passes. Full `pnpm test:e2e`: 198 passed, 9 failed, 21 skipped (10.9 min); the failures (activation, hub, sql-injection-arcade: trace-artifact ENOENT, load timeouts, a screenshot protocol error) passed alone: 35 passed, 1 skipped. Not checked: manual dev-browser and 375 px visual pass.

**INST-01 — Assigned-cohort learning analytics (instructor class results)** · Status: **Review** · Contributor: **Boey** · Branch: `inst-01-class-results` (from `origin/main` at `6d38964`, 26 October 2026, upstream unset). Approved by Boey on 26 October 2026 after a question checkpoint. Spec: [INST-01](#inst-01--assigned-cohort-learning-analytics).

- **Collision check:** no INST-01 claim, branch, route, component or test in this clone, `origin` branch names or the `admin-01-b-admin-controls` worktree (REC-01-B/ADMIN-01-B work only). `/instructor` is the COH-03 roster page (`ClassManagementPage.vue`), not a results view. Not checked: unpushed teammate work and other machines.
- **Dependencies:** builds on COH-01 and GAME-01-A, both in Review and not yet accepted (Boey's choice). G-SQL was the only production adapter when this was built (merged to `main` on 23 October 2026); Clearance, Phishing, CLI and Social now also save (SAVE-01, in Review), so class results come from attempts started in class context.
- **Decisions:** Results is a Roster | Results switch on `/instructor` (`?view=results` beside `?class=`), not a separate route. Counted: completed attempts with `context.cohortId` equal to the selected class by players who are currently active, pure-student members; pending/removed members, staff and personal practice are excluded. Best % per game reuses the LEARN-01 rule (latest completed `rulesetVersion`). Summary + per-student table + one student's paginated attempts; the spec's single-attempt detail endpoint, export and date filters are deferred. Admins use the same endpoints for any class. The spec's `GET /api/instructor/cohorts` is replaced by the existing `GET /api/cohorts/managed`. Zero denominators read "No attempts yet", never 0%. Output is labelled completion/performance evidence, not mastery. No email in results.
- **Scope:** new `server/src/modules/class-results/` (`GET /api/cohorts/:cohortId/results?page=`, `GET /api/cohorts/:cohortId/students/:studentId/attempts?gameId=&page=`; `cohort:teach` capability, session, no-store, 120/user and 240/IP per minute, 400 on unknown query keys or bad values, uniform 404 on foreign or mismatched IDs) and `client/src/features/class-results/`. **LEARN-01 — Personal progress and achievements** · Status: **Review** · Contributor: **Boey** · Branch: `learn-01-progress-history` (from `origin/main` at `2dc7724`, 26 October 2026). Approved by Boey on 26 October 2026 after a question checkpoint. Spec: [LEARN-01](#learn-01--personal-progress-and-achievements).

- **Collision check:** no LEARN-01 claim, branch or code in this clone, `origin` or the `admin-01-b-admin-controls` worktree. The only related UI is Boey's own PRO-01 sample work (`client/src/data/portalPreview.js`, unused; the empty `TrophyCase` in `Profile.vue`; the unused `GlobalProgressBar`), which Boey continues here. - **Dependency:** builds on GAME-01-A, which is in Review and not yet accepted (Boey's choice). G-SQL was the only game with saved data when this was built; the other four games now save too (SAVE-01).
- **Decisions:** achievements are derived on read from the player's completed attempts against a fixed server-side definition list, so refreshes and repeated requests cannot award twice; this replaces the spec's `AchievementAward`/`RewardEvent` collections, and the award date is the qualifying attempt's `completedAt`. Points: per game `round(100 × best completed score / maxScore)` within one `rulesetVersion`, summed across games, server-computed. All six catalog games are listed; games without a production adapter read "Results not saved yet". The spec's `GET /api/me/achievements` is folded into `GET /api/me/progress`.
- **Scope:** new `server/src/modules/progress/` (`GET /api/me/progress`, `GET /api/me/attempts?gameId=&page=`, completed attempts only, owner-scoped, no-store, rate-limited) and `client/src/features/progress/` (`ProgressPage.vue`, `ProgressSummary.vue`, `AttemptHistory.vue`, `api.js`; `TrophyCase` receives real achievements). Integration edits, all Boey-owned: a read-only list function in `server/src/modules/attempts/`, one mount in `server/src/app.js`, `/progress` in `client/src/router/index.js`, removal of the `/progress` branches in `client/src/views/Profile.vue`, and the About page's points/badges notice.
- **Implemented (26 October 2026, uncommitted on the branch):** server `server/src/modules/progress/` (`router.js`, `service.js`, `points.js`, `achievements.js`, README) with `GET /api/me/progress` and `GET /api/me/attempts?gameId=&page=` (session required, no-store, 120/user and 240/IP per minute, 400 on unknown query keys, bad `gameId` or `page`; progress reads at most the newest 1000 completed attempts); read-only `listCompletedAttempts` in `server/src/modules/attempts/service.js`; one mount in `server/src/app.js`. Client `client/src/features/progress/` (`ProgressPage.vue`, `ProgressSummary.vue`, `AttemptHistory.vue`, `api.js`, `progress.css`, README with presentation summary); `/progress` routed to it; `Profile.vue` keeps `/profile` only; `TrophyCase` receives real badges unchanged. Copy updated: About, `/profile` and register notices no longer say results are not saved. `tests/e2e/page-fit.spec.js` drops `/progress` from the fitted pages because the progress page is long by design (1229 px at 1280 × 720); its width is asserted in the new spec.

**AUTH-06 — Sign-in and session limits sized for shared addresses** · Status: **Review** · Contributor: **Boey** · Branch: `auth-05-csrf-session-limit` (follows AUTH-05, 26 October 2026). Approved by Boey on 26 October 2026 after the full E2E suite failed at setup: `main` at `6eabb84` had 168 failed and 18 passed of 212 (mostly "Could not initialize the E2E session"), and AUTH-05 had 88 failed and 91 passed. The per-IP sign-in limit counted successful sign-ins, and the session-creating CSRF bootstrap limit of 60 per 15 minutes was below one full run from 127.0.0.1. It was also below a classroom on one shared address, because anonymous CSRF sessions expire after ten minutes. Scope: `server/src/modules/auth/rate-limits.js` sets `skipSuccessfulRequests` on the per-IP sign-in limiter (30 failed sign-ins per 15 minutes; the per-account and IP failed-login budget of 5 is unchanged) and raises the session-creating CSRF bootstrap limit to `CSRF_BOOTSTRAP_LIMIT` = 300 per 15 minutes. This supersedes the 60 stated in the AUTH-05 record. Tests run on the real configuration. Tests: `server/tests/integration/auth/auth.test.js` adds 31 successful sign-ins passing and 30 failed sign-ins across accounts followed by 429; the AUTH-05 case uses the constant. Docs: auth module README. Evidence: auth integration on a replica set 26/26, three consecutive runs. One earlier run had a single failure in the existing restoration-expiry case, which did not recur; it is recorded as unexplained. Full suite `node scripts/run-with-test-db.mjs e2e --replica-set`: 181 passed, 9 failed, 22 skipped (8.2 minutes), with no rate-limit errors. The 9 remaining failures are deterministic on both projects and outside these changes: `news.spec.js:281` (newspaper audio element still connected at line 316), `portal.spec.js:12` (Back to town link click timeout at line 78), `portal.spec.js:226` (`.auth-mascot` not found), `recovery.spec.js:139` (no "Check your email" heading) and `recovery.spec.js:406` (an Activation link is visible where none is expected). Follow-up the same day (approved by Boey): diagnosis found no app regressions. `recovery.spec.js:139` hit the registration limit of 5 per hour per IP, which is now `REGISTRATION_LIMIT` = 30 so one class can register together on a shared address. Four stale or racy test lines were fixed: `news.spec.js:316` polls until the closed audio element is detached; `portal.spec.js:78` drops `exact: true` because the arcade link reads "← Back to town" (Keane's line, and Boey will tell Keane); the stale `/login` `.auth-mascot` block in `portal.spec.js` was removed (signed-in users are redirected and the real login page has no mascot); and `recovery.spec.js:448,450` use an exact Activation link label. The auth limiter tests now share one listening server, after supertest's per-request servers caused intermittent "socket hang up" failures. Auth integration passed 27/27 in four consecutive runs. Full suite: 187 passed, 3 failed, 22 skipped (7.8 minutes), with no rate-limit errors. `recovery.spec.js:518` (the same strict-mode `getByRole('status')` match fixed in cohorts) was then fixed the same way. The remaining open failure is `portal.spec.js:24` on both projects (the LLM Social Engineering Simulator card heading is not focused after returning from the arcade, possibly a focus race with the route transition). It was exposed once earlier steps passed and remains open. **Resolved (26 October 2026, approved by Boey, uncommitted on `main`):** a traced run showed the failure follows the newspaper close, not the arcade. `Dashboard.vue`'s news-to-town watcher restores newsstand focus one tick after the route changes, while the test's `toHaveURL('/')` could pass first, so the next card's heading lost focus to the late restore. Fixes: `portal.spec.js` waits for the newsstand button to be focused after closing the Times; `Dashboard.vue` skips the restore when focus is already inside a `.game-info-card`. A first guard (skip unless focus was on `body` or in the dialog) broke `news.spec.js:95`, because clicking the building focuses the town viewport (`TownMap.vue:170`) and the native dialog returns focus there, so the guard was narrowed to the card. Evidence: `node scripts/run-with-test-db.mjs e2e --replica-set --workers=1 --project=desktop-chromium --project=mobile-chromium tests/e2e/portal.spec.js tests/e2e/news.spec.js` 36 passed, 0 failed; ESLint passed on both files; Prettier differences in both files predate this change. Full suite `node scripts/run-with-test-db.mjs e2e --replica-set`: 190 passed, 0 failed, 22 skipped (6.7 minutes).

**AUTH-05 — CSRF bootstrap limit counts only new sessions** · Status: **Review** · Contributor: **Boey** · Branch: `auth-05-csrf-session-limit` from `coh-03-instructor-class-url` at `2ab7a39` (26 October 2026). Approved by Boey on 26 October 2026 after diagnosing intermittent E2E failures: `GET /api/auth/csrf` allowed 60 requests per 15 minutes per IP, and a single admin-workspace plus cohorts run from 127.0.0.1 exceeded it (a trace showed the 10th desktop cohorts bootstrap returning 429, then `/api/auth/me` 401). The same budget could also block a grader or instructor working locally, because feature modules fetch a token before most mutations. Scope: `server/src/modules/auth/router.js` skips the CSRF limiter when the request already has a live session with a token (the same condition under which the route only returns the existing token without writing). Requests that create or replace a session still use the unchanged limiter in `rate-limits.js` (60 per 15 minutes); tests run on the real configuration. Also fixes the strict-mode `getByRole('status')` match at `tests/e2e/cohorts.spec.js` (approval notice plus the roster loading status). Tests: new integration case in `server/tests/integration/auth/auth.test.js` (61 reuses on one session pass; the 61st new-session bootstrap returns 429; reuse still passes afterwards). Docs: auth module README. No README "Using the app" change (no user-visible control changes). Note for Russell: the comment at `tests/e2e/clearance-tower.spec.js:14` describes the old per-load budget; his file is unchanged. Evidence: `node scripts/run-with-test-db.mjs integration --replica-set server/tests/integration/auth/ --no-file-parallelism` 24/24 passed; focused ESLint and Prettier clean (the auth README already failed Prettier on `main` and was not reformatted). E2E: `node scripts/run-with-test-db.mjs e2e --replica-set tests/e2e/admin-workspace.spec.js tests/e2e/cohorts.spec.js --workers=1` three times: cohorts 6/6 passed (mobile and desktop); admin-workspace 5/6, with one desktop `page.waitForResponse` timeout at `tests/e2e/admin-workspace.spec.js:23`. Follow-up (26 October 2026, test-only, approved by Boey): that timeout came after a status click. `AccountStatusPanel.vue` reloads the list when a status change completes and aborts any list request in flight (`loadUsers`), so a filter request sent before the change settled could be cancelled and never answer. The suspected Role/Status ordering was ruled out (the wait is registered before Apply). A `changeAccountStatus` helper in `tests/e2e/admin-workspace.spec.js` now waits for the PATCH and the panel's list reload at all three status clicks. The failing trace had been cleared, so the cause is inferred from the code and the stall point. After the fix, the same pair passed 12/12 over three runs (admin-workspace and cohorts, mobile and desktop).

**COH-03 — Keep the selected class in the instructor address** · Status: **Review** · Contributor: **Boey** · Branch: `coh-03-instructor-class-url` from `main` at `6eabb84` (26 October 2026). Approved by Boey on 26 October 2026 after diagnosing the `tests/e2e/cohorts.spec.js` failure: after `page.reload()` the instructor page fell back to the first assigned class, and leftover classes from earlier specs sorted first, so the order-dependent test looked at the wrong class. Scope: `client/src/features/cohorts/pages/ClassManagementPage.vue` reads `?class=<cohortId>` on load and writes the selected class back with `history.replaceState` (not a router navigation, so `PortalLayout.vue`'s route watcher does not move focus and `scrollBehavior` does not scroll on a class switch); an unknown or unassigned ID falls back to the first assigned class and the address is corrected. The ID only preselects the dropdown; server authorization is unchanged. Tests: two cases in `client/src/features/cohorts/__tests__/management.test.js` (reload keeps the class; unknown ID falls back); the E2E spec now asserts the class survives the reload. Docs: cohorts README and README "Using the app" (COH-01 paragraph). Collision check: Boey-owned cohorts module; no shared files, schemas or other modules touched. Evidence: `pnpm exec vitest run --project client client/src/features/cohorts` 20/20 passed; focused ESLint and Prettier clean. `node scripts/run-with-test-db.mjs e2e --replica-set tests/e2e/admin-workspace.spec.js tests/e2e/cohorts.spec.js --workers=1` over three runs: the class-join test passed on mobile 3/3 and on desktop-only once; the remaining failures are intermittent and occur before or outside the changed step (student `/classes` showing sign-in at spec line 128, a missing approval status at line 135, and admin-workspace setup at lines 14 and 23). Those flakes are unresolved and not caused by this change. Limit: an assigned class beyond the first page of 50 falls back to page one.

**HUB-01 — 3D hub landing page** · P1 · Status: **Review** · Contributor/reviewer: **Boey** · Branch: `hub-01-3d-landing` (from `origin/main` at `f5f8995`).

- **Approval / scope:** Boey, as repository owner, approved on 20 October 2026 and the team agreed. `/` (`Dashboard.vue`) renders a Babylon.js 3D hub, the "Thames Wharf" (painterly golden-hour 1880s London wharf, Sharlock the capybara detective as the player), ported from Boey's local engine spike. Each building is a destination that links to the existing game route from `client/src/data/gameCatalog.js`, which is read-only here (no catalog edits). Buildings: `cli-cyber-defender` = Telegraph Office; `phishing-post-mortem` = Royal Mail Post Office; `sql-injection-arcade` = H.M. Customs House; `threat-briefing` = Newsstand on the pier; `social-engineering-simulator` = The Capybara & Crown pub; `clearance-tower` (replaced `regex-defender`) = Thames River Police station with a watchtower signed "Clearance Tower".
- **Concept / complexity:** ★★★ implementation complexity; WebGL scene, follow camera, physics, lifecycle disposal and accessibility twins. No gameplay, scoring, API or schema change.
- **Camera / modes:** one camera model per mounted mode. The Babylon follow camera replaces Panzoom on `/`. The Panzoom pixel town moves to `client/src/features/legacy-town/` and `Dashboard.vue` renders it instead of the 3D hub (never both mounted) when the user picks "2D mode" or WebGL is unavailable. Rollback tag: `legacy-town-2026-10` at `f5f8995`.
- **Requirements kept:** reduced motion; full dispose of engine, scene, listeners and audio on leave; DOM destination twins with `data-testid`s for keyboard, screen-reader and E2E use; quality tiers (desktop and compact; the low tier and mobile pass are follow-ups); mascot and avatar identities kept (navbar 3D logo, pixel capybaras elsewhere); audio muted by default with no persisted unmuted preference.
- **Owned paths:** `client/src/features/hub/`, `client/src/features/legacy-town/`, `client/public/hub/`, `client/src/views/Dashboard.vue`, `tests/e2e/hub.spec.js`, `tests/e2e/portal.spec.js`, `news.spec.js`, `cli-cyber-defender.spec.js`, `phishing-post-mortem.spec.js` and `clearance-tower.spec.js` (2D-mode init script only), `client/package.json` and `pnpm-lock.yaml` (dependencies only), `client/vite.config.js` (only if Babylon/Havok need it), `client/src/main.js` (only if the `town.css` import moves), TASKS.md, DESIGN.md and agents.md (README.md only for a direct contradiction). No other shared file; the catalog, router, `PortalLayout.vue`/`Navbar.vue` and `main.css` are not edited.
- **Dependencies:** client `@babylonjs/core` **9.31.0** (Apache-2.0) and `@babylonjs/havok` **1.3.14** (free licence, verified at install), both pinned exact via pnpm and the root lockfile. `three` is not authorized.
- **Collision evidence (20 October 2026):** no branch, commit or code on any ref mentions Babylon, a 3D hub, multiplayer or sockets; the town components are unchanged upstream since `5234720`; UI-06 is taken (skipped hover cards), hence HUB-01. A clean search cannot prove that teammates' unpushed work does not exist. Upstream changes the hub must respect: Clearance Tower replacing Regex Defender in the catalog and router, the pixel-wipe page transitions (`installPageWipes` in `client/src/main.js`) and the sidebar `hideAccountSummary` route meta.

**HUB-02 — Automatic 3D/2D choice and graphics quality** · P1 · Status: **Review** · Contributor/reviewer: **Boey** · Branch: `hub-02-auto-quality` (from `hub-01-3d-landing` at `e588d71`).

Adds a Graphics setting (Auto/High/Medium/Low, `sharlock.graphics`) with a new low tier (no shadows, mirror or bloom, 0.75 render scale, fewer boats/workers/gulls, 30 fps cap); instant device checks (`client/src/features/hub/deviceCheck.js`: no WebGL2 → locked 2D; software renderer → automatic 2D; save-data, ≤2 GB memory or ≤2 cores → low tier); a runtime watch on Auto (`perfWatch.js`: 1 s warm-up, 4 s median fps, thresholds 40 desktop/compact and 20 low, steps desktop → compact → low → 2D, remembered in `sharlock.hubPerf`, never auto-switching to 2D after an explicit 3D choice); a dismissable automatic-2D notice with "Try 3D anyway" behind a warning dialog (`legacy-town/Hub3dWarning.vue`); and an About page section `#graphics`.
- **Owned paths:** `client/src/features/hub/`, `client/src/features/legacy-town/`, `client/src/views/Dashboard.vue`, `client/src/features/about/AboutStory.vue` (one section), `tests/e2e/hub.spec.js`, `tests/e2e/hub-auto.spec.js`, TASKS.md. No dependency, router, catalog or shared-style change.

**HUB-04 — Sharlock character pass (proportions, outfit, idles and jump)** · P2 · Status: **Review** · Contributor/reviewer: **Boey** · Branch: `hub-04-sharlock-character` (from `main` at `df82f73`).

- **Approval / scope:** Boey approved on 22 October 2026 (HUB-TODO §10 and §13), done in one pass. Sharlock only (the dock workers are unchanged and use a separate crowd builder). Design: Sharlock keeps his original face, eyes, ears and colours, with targeted proportion tweaks Boey asked for after the Jellycat "Clyde Capybara" plush: a stubbier head that blends into the body through a tapered neck, a slightly pear-shaped body that is fuller at the rear, bigger and flatter nostrils, and an upright Jellycat-style sit on a wider rump. The outfit is a smaller deerstalker, the monocle and a knitted red scarf in place of the full cape. Two redesigns were tried and rejected by Boey on 22 October 2026 (an exaggerated cute pass with big eyes, blush and a tail, then a realistic plush-style capybara), so the original look was restored. Idles: blinks, ear wiggle every ~4–9 s, sniff every ~5–10 s, looks toward the nearest door within 6 m when standing still, sits down after 8 s still and yawns every ~6–10 s while sitting; any movement stands him up. Reduced motion halves sit and tilt, keeps blinks, and makes the yawn eyes-only. Jump: Space on desktop (not while focus is on a button, link or input) and a "Jump" touch button next to the joystick; apex about 0.65 m; no double jump; the camera does not bob; reduced motion keeps the jump without squash and stretch. A small happy hop plays when entering a building (skipped under reduced motion). Owned paths: `client/src/features/hub/`, `tests/e2e/hub.spec.js`, README "Using the app", TASKS.md. No dependency, router, catalog, shared-style or server change. Out of scope: squeak sounds (audio) and cosmetics (skins).

**G-CLEAR — Clearance Tower (replaces G-REGEX)** · P1 · Status: **Review** · Contributor: **Russell** · Branch: `g-clear-clearance-tower`

- **Exact approval and scope:** on 15 October 2026 Russell selected Regex Defender, then asked for a more visual, engaging replacement for university learners and chose Clearance Tower, an access-control policy game reusing the shed. Russell approved the written plan with two changes: the town's light palette (no dark colour exception) and Pixelify Sans, and asked that the code stay plainly readable. Decisions: intelligence-agency setting throughout; six levels, two per act (discretionary, Bell-LaPadula, role-based), no finale; predictions before each simulated day count towards the score.
- **Concept / difficulty:** ★★★ learner difficulty. Learners design an access policy, predict flows, then watch a simulated day scored on leaks, blocked work, least privilege and predictions. Source: `07-access-control.pptx` slides 4–7, 13–22, 24–25, 26–31, 32 and 34–38.
- **Feature files:** `server/src/modules/games/clearance-tower/` (`levels.js`, `models.js`, `simulate.js`, `scoring.js`, `policy.js`, `service.js`, `router.js`, `README.md`); `client/src/games/clearance-tower/` (root, components, composables, request wrapper, `clearance.css`, `README.md`, `__tests__/`); `server/tests/unit/clearance-tower/`, `server/tests/integration/clearance-tower/`, `tests/e2e/clearance-tower.spec.js`.
- **Exact shared integrations:** replace the `regex-defender` catalog entry in place (same shed at (720, 496), difficulty 3; `isPreview: false` only after verification); retarget the `red-blue` redirect and add a `regex-defender` redirect to `/games/clearance-tower`, plus one lazy route; one import and mount in `server/src/app.js`; update `Town.test.js`, `portal.spec.js` and add a `game-palette.test.js` entry; synchronize README/TASKS blueprints (G-REGEX recorded as superseded), DESIGN.md and the agents.md preview wording. Russell's own placeholder `client/src/games/regex-defender/test.txt` is removed.
- **Dependencies:** existing Vue/Express scaffold, shared Axios client and town registration only. No new dependency, database, external service or private environment change. Unsaved interim endpoints, like G-CLI/G-PHISH, until GAME-01 exists.
- **Collision evidence (15 October 2026):** `main` at `b33f241` matched `origin/main`; no `clearance`, access-matrix, Bell-LaPadula or role-based game code on main, `origin/auth-01-account-sessions` (Boey's AUTH/COH-01 work, which owns real accounts, cohorts and enrolment and was not touched) or `origin/code-readability-review`. Regex traces were only Boey's catalog preview, generic preview, legacy redirect and test assertions, plus Russell's placeholder. Cohort enrolment (COH-01) is Boey's existing work and the cohort leaderboard (BOARD-01) is blocked by GAME-01, so neither is in scope. Teammates' machines and PR contents were not inspected (no `gh` CLI). (superseded: GAME-01-A is on main; BOARD-01 claimed 27 October 2026, see In Progress)
- **Coordination:** the shed entry and G-REGEX blueprint come from Boey's town work; Russell was advised to give Boey a heads-up before this reaches main.
- **Delivered:** six playable cases with server-owned day scripts, prediction answers, simulation and scoring; animated tower, duty log, play/pause/step/skip and debrief with prediction review and slide-referenced takeaways. Catalog entry now `isPreview: false`.   **REC-01-B — Student-only staff-assisted password recovery** · Status: **Review** · Contributor: **Boey** · Branch: `auth-01-account-sessions` · Approved scope baseline: `ba6a767417badbb9ea34e67b51fb9e563b3bee36` · P0 · ★★★ implementation complexity.

- **Approval / scope:** Boey approved the rebuild plan on 16 October 2026. Recover active, pure-student accounts after school-channel identity verification outside the app. The staff checkbox records attestation only; collect no uploads or sensitive free text. Staff privately deliver a once-visible reset link, and the student chooses a password. All issuers with the admin role use the admin area and may help any active pure student, with or without classes, when authorized by `admin:recovery:manage`. Instructor roster recovery controls are available only to issuers with the instructor role and no admin role; instructors may help only while the student has active membership in an active class/institution assigned to that active instructor. Reject staff and dual-role targets. Emergency recovery remains deferred.
- **Exact interfaces:** `POST /admin/recovery/lookup` accepts exactly `{ email }` and returns `{ student: { id, username, email, avatarId } }` or `{ student: null }` using an exact registered-email match. `POST /cohorts/:cohortId/students/:studentId/recovery` and `POST /admin/users/:userId/recovery` each accept exactly `{ identityVerified: true }` and return `{ resetUrl, expiresAt }` once. `POST /auth/reset-password` accepts exactly `{ token, password }` and returns `204` without establishing a session. All paths are under `/api`; feature wrappers omit the prefix. Staff routes reuse the recovery router and session/mutation middleware; public reset is mounted inside the existing anonymous auth session/origin/CSRF runtime. The reset page must synchronously capture and scrub the URL fragment through the router before session restoration or requests. LoginPage includes the recovery help notice. User-facing password guidance says “at least 12 characters”; never say “12–128 characters”; the backend retains the 128-character maximum. Any additional existing public auth-page copy cleanup is limited to password-range wording and must be identified in the frontend inventory. See handover §§30–54 for the full UI, eligibility and reset-page contract.
- **Token / transaction / concurrency:** reuse `PasswordReset` with purpose `student-password-reset`; generate 32 random bytes/base64url, store only SHA-256, expire after 30 minutes, and build `/reset-password#token=...` from trusted configured frontend origin, never Host. Reissue revokes only outstanding student-reset tokens for that user/purpose. At issuance and redemption recheck active pure-student target and active authorized issuer; instructor redemption also rechecks exact active membership, assignment and active parents. Atomically consume with password-hash/passwordChangedAt update, `authVersion` increment, deletion of target sessions, revocation of remaining student-reset tokens and metadata-only audit; audit failure rolls back mutation. Add internal conditional `recoveryRevision` guards to User, Membership and InstructorAssignment for contention/retry/fresh checks; no exposure, migration or new collection. Charge the 3-per-student/30-minute quota only after issuer, target and relationship guards pass; an eligible attempt counts even if later token, audit or commit work fails, and callback retries count once per request. Unauthorized/ineligible attempts do not charge the shared student bucket. No-store, exact allowlists, safe filters, bounded bodies, read-only GETs and generic failures; failed recovery never locks accounts. Rates: 10 issuances/staff/15 min and 3/student/30 min; lookup 30/admin/15 min; redemption 10/IP/15 min, reusing process-local limiter infrastructure. No new dependency. See handover §§39–62 for remaining approved details.
- **Dependencies / existing contracts:** AUTH-02 and COH-01 are available foundations; COH-02 is a separately approved prerequisite that adds only the authorized instructor roster's `studentId` projection. Coordinate LOG-01 audit integration (the session, cohort, audit, permission and activation services/models already exist in Review). Preserve activation's existing 24-hour behavior. No admin/cohort router edits or startup/migration changes are planned.
- **Declared paths:** server module `server/src/modules/recovery/{model.js,policy.js,service.js,router.js,README.md,guards.js}`; shared server `server/src/app.js`, `server/src/modules/auth/router.js`, `server/src/modules/auth/rate-limits.js`, `server/src/services/permissions.js`, `server/src/modules/audit/policy.js`, `server/src/models/User.js`, `Membership.js`, `InstructorAssignment.js`; server tests `server/tests/unit/recovery/password-reset.test.js`, `server/tests/integration/recovery/password-reset.test.js`, and `server/tests/integration/auth/auth.test.js` (two approved deterministic login/session-touch race tests only; no auth/session production-code changes). COH-02 prerequisite paths remain separately approved: `server/src/modules/cohorts/service.js`, `server/tests/integration/cohorts/enrollment.test.js`. Client feature `client/src/features/recovery/{README.md,api.js,recovery.css,components/RecoveryIssueForm.vue,components/RecoveryStudentLookup.vue,components/RecoveryLinkDialog.vue,components/ResetPasswordForm.vue,pages/ResetPasswordPage.vue,__tests__/api.test.js,__tests__/recovery.test.js}`; shared client `client/src/router/index.js` (synchronous fragment capture/scrub), `client/src/features/cohorts/pages/ClassManagementPage.vue`, `client/src/features/cohorts/components/EnrollmentQueue.vue`, `client/src/features/cohorts/__tests__/components.test.js`, `client/src/features/cohorts/__tests__/management.test.js`, `client/src/features/admin/pages/AdminPage.vue`, `client/src/features/admin/__tests__/admin.test.js`, `client/src/features/auth/pages/LoginPage.vue` (recovery help notice), `client/src/features/auth/components/RegisterForm.vue`, `client/src/features/activation/pages/ActivationPage.vue`, `client/src/features/auth/api.js`, and `client/src/features/auth/__tests__/auth.test.js` (password-copy/feedback and matching assertions only; no validation-behavior changes); browser test `tests/e2e/recovery.spec.js`. Documentation: this record, minimal README wording within its five sections, lowercase `agents.md` rule clarification, and server/client recovery READMEs. Existing recovery storage/initializer/indexes suffice. Preserve activation behavior; no `client/src/main.js`, auth-store, global CSS, Navbar or PortalLayout changes.

**ADMIN-01-A / REC-01-A — Real staff/class workspace and activation** · Status: **Review** · ★★★ implementation complexity · Contributor/reviewer: **Boey** · Branch: `auth-01-account-sessions`.

- **Approved scope / journey:** Boey approved the two-scope plan on 15 October 2026, explicitly excluding demo seeding. An administrator creates an institution/class, provisions a separate disabled instructor, delivers a once-visible 24-hour activation link privately, then assigns the activated instructor to active classes. The instructor chooses a policy-compliant password and signs in normally. Reissuing a link supersedes old links; stale assignment edits are rejected. The first administrator's normal-database creation is explicitly authorized, with details kept out of project source/notes.
- **Declared flow / files:** new admin `{policy,service,router}` files beside the preserved bootstrap code; feature-owned `server/src/modules/recovery/{model,policy,service,router,storage}.js` and README for activation only; new `client/src/features/admin/` API/page/components/styles/tests and `client/src/features/activation/` form/page/API/styles/tests. Existing `/admin` preview is replaced in place and `/activate-account` added. Minimal shared integrations: User instructor-assignment revision, auth runtime reusable mutation protection/activation registration, app/startup, permission capabilities, typed audit enums, router/guard, one role-aware PortalLayout navigation entry, and root/module documentation. `main.js` defers activation-entry restoration until fragment removal; existing `scripts/lib/mongodb.js` permits its opt-in replica flag for E2E as well as integration, with standalone defaults preserved. The existing E2E auth fixture appends required composition to its random synthetic password. Reuse existing User/class/assignment models, shared Axios and current session/CSRF/error contracts. No new dependency, duplicate identity store, global CSS/game/llm edits or seeds.
- **Contract / defaults:** admin GET lists default 20/max 50; POST institution `{name,slug}`, cohort `{institutionId,name,term}`, instructor `{email,username,avatarId}`; POST instructor activation reissue with empty body; PUT assignments `{cohortIds,expectedRevision}` capped 50, active references only. POST `/api/auth/activate-account` accepts exactly `{token,password}`; confirmation remains client-local. Tokens are random 32-byte capabilities with hash-only storage, 24-hour server expiry, atomic consumption and private URL fragments cleared from address/local state. New instructors are disabled until activation, never implicitly promoted from existing accounts. Version guard serializes assignment mutations without logging teachers out for ordinary assignment edits. Audit failures roll back writes.
- **UI / reuse:** `/admin` keeps existing utility presentation and branded pixel shell; activation is a shared public pixel page using existing tokens, labels/focus/feedback and local styles. Reuse AvatarPicker, PortalLayout/Navbar, PixelIcon and shared controls. No navbar/town redesign, card/camera/media change or ownership takeover. Incoming main `90000d7` adds Eric's UI fit/palette work; source/policy was inspected and will be preserved for later explicitly approved integration.
- **Collision / planned checks:** on auth at `3d4ec07`, no existing admin writers/activation implementation or active claim; current User/class/auth/audit/preview interfaces are approved reuse points. Only our approved AUTH-01-C edits and preserved Finder files may change. Main owns security/activation/transactions/shared integration; bounded admin backend and client work may be delegated with disjoint ownership. Test direct foreign IDs/roles, duplicate provision, active-reference checks, stale/concurrent assignments, token expiry/reissue/reuse/concurrency and rollback, plus actual admin/instructor browser journey. Use guarded disposable transaction tests and narrow rendered checks. Recheck, implement, record evidence, move to Review and publish auth only; no main merge/PR. General recovery, enrollment, policy/status/learning dashboards remain outside this slice.

Initial event enum is `admin.bootstrap`; outcomes are success/refused/failure. This slice emits only the committed bootstrap success; refusals/errors use fixed terminal guidance. Operator actorId is null, never an invented account. Writer owns occurrence/expiry time. No free-text metadata, passwords/hashes/codes/tokens/cookies/URIs/bodies/stacks.

**AUTH-01-A — Backend account/session foundation and capybara avatar presets** · Status: **Review** · Contributor/reviewer: **Boey** · Branch: `auth-01-account-sessions` · ★★★ implementation complexity.

- **Exact approval / settled decisions:** Boey replied “otherwise, approved” to the concrete AUTH-01-A backend plan and requested generated capybara avatars. Registration creates only a student and signs them in automatically. The eventual account-first UX and one-time optional class-code onboarding remain the agreed direction; the guest trial was withdrawn. This backend slice leaves current UI/game access unchanged until separately approved integration.
- **Affected paths / minimal integrations:** new `server/src/models/User.js`; feature-owned `server/src/modules/auth/` validation, avatar definitions, password, session, CSRF, rate-limits, storage, service, router and README; minimal auth wiring in `server/src/app.js` and `server/src/server.js`; new `server/tests/unit/auth/` and `server/tests/integration/auth/`, including reusable disposable-test helpers; only app construction/auth disposal changes in the existing scaffold, News, Phishing and CLI integration test files. Dependency changes are limited to `server/package.json`, `pnpm-workspace.yaml` and the root `pnpm-lock.yaml`. New assets and prompt/ID documentation live in `client/public/images/avatars/`; DESIGN.md receives an asset credit. TASKS.md and README.md record truthful backend-only readiness. No client application code, game module, private environment or cancelled Dash path is included.
- **Dependencies / policy constants:** reuse Node 24.12.0, pnpm 12.3.4, the existing Mongoose connection and validated environment/request-error interfaces. Add pinned Argon2, express-session, connect-mongo, csrf-sync and express-rate-limit plus the matching existing Mongo driver peer; allow only Argon2's native installation alongside the existing esbuild allowance. Passwords are unchanged, well-formed Unicode strings of 15–128 code points; email is trimmed/lowercased ASCII, maximum 254 characters; username is trimmed, 3–24 ASCII letters/digits/underscores/hyphens with case-insensitive uniqueness. Six avatar IDs are `mint`, `blue`, `peach`, `detective`, `reader`, `gamer`. Argon2id uses 19 MiB, two iterations, parallelism one. Authenticated sessions expire after 30 idle minutes or eight absolute hours; anonymous CSRF bootstrap expires after ten minutes. Login limits: 30 requests/IP/15 minutes plus five failed email/IP attempts/15 minutes; registration: five/IP/hour; CSRF bootstrap: 60/IP/15 minutes. At most two simultaneous password operations, with no unbounded queue. HTTP cookies are permitted only by validated localhost development/test configuration.
- **Data/security boundary:** bind User and `auth_sessions` storage to the existing database; create required collections/indexes without drops, migrations, destructive index synchronization or seeding. Recheck account status/authVersion on authenticated requests. Apply CSRF and exact Origin/Referer checks to auth mutations, explicit safe user serialization, session rotation, expiry and revocation, with no MemoryStore fallback or secret/body logging. Keep auth lifecycle data server-owned and avoid concurrent requests recreating revoked sessions. **CODE-09 — Mobile offcanvas lifecycle audit/proposal** · Status: **Review** · Complexity: ★★☆ lifecycle review; ★☆☆ proposed naming-only change · Contributor/reviewer: **Boey** · Branch: `code-readability-review`.

- **Flow / invariants:** mount creates the existing Bootstrap Offcanvas and 768px desktop query. Show/hide events update `menuOpen` at transition start; the completed hidden event restores page/news heading focus after navigation or desktop resize, otherwise the Navbar opener. Crossing into desktop closes the drawer and crossing back does not reopen it. Unmount removes the media/element listeners and disposes the instance. Preserve this code's event timing, branches, listener identities and cleanup, without claiming a new runtime verification.
- **Proposed single naming-only batch, awaiting approval:** in PortalLayout.vue rename `media` to `desktopQuery`, `showState` to `markMenuOpen`, `hideState` to `markMenuClosed`, `onHidden` to `restoreFocusAfterMenuCloses`, and `onBreakpoint` to `closeMenuOnDesktop`, including their existing registration/removal references. These names identify the responsive condition and each callback's job. Keep the state updates on the same show/hide events; the mark names describe the boolean state, not completion of the visual transition. Record approval/results here. No new helpers, source files, branches or behavior changes are proposed.
- **Exact boundaries / shared design:** edit only those five private identifiers in `client/src/layouts/PortalLayout.vue` and the task record in TASKS.md. **G-SOC — LLM Social Engineering Simulator (playable MVP)** · P1 · Status: **Review** · Contributor: **Eric** · Branch: `codex/g-soc-simulator-mvp`

- **Exact approval and scope:** Eric approved the playable MVP. Scope is the playable simulator that reuses the existing `social-engineering-simulator` catalog entry, route and `office` building at (432, 520); its ID, name, description, difficulty, background knowledge, coordinates, sprite and icon are unchanged. A fictional LLM-played colleague pressures the player for a synthetic reset code; the server holds the code and system prompts and scores everything. Stateless and in-memory only, no Mongo and no persistence; the debrief is labelled "practice — not saved".
- **Concept / complexity:** ★★☆ learner difficulty; ★★☆ implementation complexity. Social engineering, impersonation, urgency, reset-code confidentiality and independent verification.
- **Guarded engine (original 11 October implementation report):** the report stated that `server/src/modules/games/social-engineering-simulator/llm/` (adapter, Ollama/cloud providers, `chatSessionStore`, `chatSessionRunner`, `llmError`) was another contributor's work (Torchiki), and that the game imported it unchanged without editing, moving or rebuilding files in `llm/`. The later source comparison below qualifies that statement against current main.
- **Later source comparison (13 October 2026):** comparing the pre-integration HEAD with verified main shows `llm/adapter.js`, `llm/chatSessionRunner.js` and `llm/providers/cloud.js` differ. The reviewed `cloud.js` implements Gemini against Google’s Generative Language API and defaults to `gemini-2.5-flash`. This comparison does not establish who changed those files or whether the game uses that provider at runtime; provider configuration and live-model behavior were not inspected. Preserve the original implementation report above as dated history, do not edit the guarded engine, and do not infer contributor consent or live verification.
- **Feature files (added):** server module root `server/src/modules/games/social-engineering-simulator/` (`scenarios.js`, `policy.js`, `service.js`, `router.js`, `README.md`) beside the guarded `llm/`; server tests `server/tests/unit/social-engineering-simulator/` (`scenarios.test.js`, `policy.test.js`, `service.test.js`, `router.test.js`). Client module `client/src/games/social-engineering-simulator/` (`SocialEngineeringSimulator.vue`, `components/ScenarioBrief.vue`, `components/ChatThread.vue`, `components/MessageComposer.vue`, `components/DecisionBar.vue`, `components/DebriefPanel.vue`, `composables/useSocialEngineeringSession.js`, `simulator.js`, `sim.css`, `__tests__/session.test.js`, `__tests__/components.test.js`).
- **Interim endpoints (no persistence):** `POST /api/games/social-engineering-simulator/session` returns `{ sessionId, persona, brief, openingMessage }`; `POST .../message` takes `{ sessionId, content:1..1000 }` and returns `{ reply, turnsUsed, turnBudget, leaked, ended }`; `POST .../complete` takes `{ sessionId, decision: refuse|verify-independently|share-code }` and returns `{ score, maxScore, outcome, breakdown[], debrief, saved:false }`. All send `Cache-Control: no-store`. Errors use `{ error:{ code, message, fieldErrors? }, requestId }`: `SOC_INVALID_BODY` (400), `LLM_SESSION_NOT_FOUND` (404), `SOC_RATE_LIMITED` (429), adapter `LLM_*` (503), `SOC_UNAVAILABLE` (503). The module README records the GAME-01 migration path.
- **Scoring (server-authoritative):** max 100 — `noLeak` 60 (0 and outcome `compromised` if the code leaks or the player chooses share-code), `verify` 25 (only for verify-independently; refuse alone 0; with the real-colleague chat on, it follows contact instead, see the G-SOC real-colleague record below), `efficiency` 15 within the turn budget, scaling to 0 beyond it. Outcome is `resisted` / `partial` / `compromised`. Leak detection normalises (uppercase, strip spaces/hyphens) and substring-matches; a leaked message is never forwarded to the model. Content is synthetic: fictional `SYNTH-RESET-NNNN` codes and `.example` names, server-owned prompts, bounded transcripts reusing the guarded store (20-message bound, 30-minute idle TTL).
- **Exact shared integrations (four files):** server `app.js` (import + one `createSocialEngineeringRouter(socialEngineering)` mount, options extended to `{ news, phishing, socialEngineering }`); server `server.js` (build the adapter from parsed env via `createLlmAdapter` and pass `socialEngineering: { service: createSocialEngineeringService({ adapter }) }`); client `router/index.js` (one lazy `/games/social-engineering-simulator` route ahead of the `/games/:gameId` catch-all); client `gameCatalog.js` (`isPreview: true → false` on the one entry, nothing else). The catalog flip forced two consequential test updates: the readiness assertion in `client/src/__tests__/Town.test.js` and the playable/redirect branches in `tests/e2e/portal.spec.js` (same pattern G-PHISH recorded). No other shared file was edited; the guarded `llm/` directory and the four other game previews are untouched.
- **Dependencies:** existing Vue/Express scaffold, shared Axios client, town registration and the guarded `llm/` engine only. `rulesetVersion: '2026-10-11'`. No new dependency, no lockfile change, no database, no private environment change. If no LLM is configured the adapter reports `configured:false` and the endpoints fail gracefully with `LLM_NOT_CONFIGURED`. AUTH-01/AUTH-02/DATA-01/GAME-01 are not dependencies and are not implemented here; documented seams let GAME-01 adopt it without a rewrite.
- **Collision evidence:** rechecked a clean worktree at `952c048` immediately before editing. The only pre-existing `social-engineering-simulator` traces were the guarded `llm/` directory and its six tests (Torchiki, `663399f`/`3ec5ead`/`df62346`), the catalog entry, the `Town.test.js` ID/readiness assertions and the `access-control` legacy redirect. No module-root game files, no `scenarios`/`policy`/`service`/`router` under that path, no client `games/social-engineering-simulator/` directory and no competing claim were found. Unfetched remote changes, live PRs, separately managed NPC work and teammates' machines were not inspected; a clean local tree does not prove global availability.
- **Verification (11 October 2026, this worktree):** `pnpm lint` — clean. `pnpm test:unit` — **195 passed across 24 files** (36 new: 22 server across the four SOC unit files, plus 14 client across the composable and component suites; adapter mocked, no live LLM). `pnpm format:check` — all files match Prettier. `pnpm build` — succeeded in ~409ms, emitting the route's own lazy chunks (`SocialEngineeringSimulator` 10.45 kB JS / 6.58 kB CSS), and a `client/dist` scan for `SYNTH-RESET`, persona names and `systemPrompt` returned **zero matches**. `pnpm test:integration` — **28 passed across 3 files** on a disposable in-memory `_test` Mongo (existing news/phishing/scaffold suites unaffected by the additive `app.js` mount). `pnpm test:e2e tests/e2e/portal.spec.js` (after `pnpm exec playwright install chromium`) — **20 passed** across mobile and desktop Chromium, including entering the now-playable office from the town card and the `access-control` redirect landing on the playable game.

The turn budget (8), persona set (four starters) and `WEIGHTS` are tunable MVP defaults.

**G-SOC — Real-colleague verification (two chats, two LLM roles)** · P1 · Status: **Review (merged to `main` via PR #3, `c46dfba`)** · Contributor: **Eric** · Branch: `g-soc-real-colleague`

- **Approved scope:** Eric approved the design on 15 October 2026 (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-15-soc-real-colleague-verification-design.md`, plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-15-soc-real-colleague-verification.md`). The one-click verify choice becomes a second chat with a real colleague played by Ollama; the bad actor stays on Gemini. Scoring stays server-authoritative. No persistence, no new dependency, no lockfile change, no database.
- **Implemented behaviour:** two adapters with fixed roles (`llmConfig.js`); four colleagues and three scripted nudges per persona (`verifiers.js`); `POST /api/games/social-engineering-simulator/verifier-message` and `POST .../nudge`; 5 colleague replies per run, one colleague reply in flight server-wide, `SOC_VERIFIER_LIMIT` (409) and `SOC_VERIFIER_BUSY` (429); the 25 verify points follow contact, awarded for `refuse` (or the still-accepted `verify-independently`) after a colleague reply when the run is not compromised, and the verify decision button is removed in colleague mode (changed 16 October 2026 at Eric's request; `RULESET_VERSION` still `2026-10-15`); 60 s server (setting range 1000 to 60000 ms) and 65 s browser colleague timeouts via `llmTimeout.js` without editing `llm/`; the colleague reply is trimmed to at most 3 sentences with URLs removed (sentences split on whitespace after `.`, `!` or `?`, so dotted hostnames and version numbers stay intact), and delivered nudges are added to the bad actor's next prompt; fallback to the old one-click verify when `LLM_COLLEAGUE_BASE_URL` is unset. `LLM_BASE_URL` and `LLM_AUTH_TOKEN` are no longer read by the game.
- **Affected paths:** server module `server/src/modules/games/social-engineering-simulator/` (`llmConfig.js`, `llmTimeout.js`, `verifiers.js` added; `policy.js`, `router.js`, `service.js`, `README.md` changed); `server/src/config/env.js` and `server/src/server.js`; `server/.env.example` (the four `LLM_COLLEAGUE_*` names, token intentionally empty); server tests under `server/tests/unit/` (`environment.test.js`, `social-engineering-simulator/llm-config.test.js`, `verifiers.test.js` added; `policy`, `router`, `service` tests changed); client module `client/src/games/social-engineering-simulator/` (`components/ConversationTabs.vue`, `composables/useVerifierChat.js`, `__tests__/verifier.test.js` added; `SocialEngineeringSimulator.vue`, `simulator.js`, `sim.css`, `ScenarioBrief.vue`, `DecisionBar.vue`, `DebriefPanel.vue`, `MessageComposer.vue`, `useSocialEngineeringSession.js` and two test files changed); `README.md`, `TASKS.md`, `DESIGN.md` and the spec and plan under `docs/superpowers/`.
- **Dependencies:** the existing guarded `llm/` engine (imported, unchanged), a Gemini key for the bad actor and a reachable Ollama gateway for the colleague. The gateway token is shared privately by the project owner and kept only in each teammate's own `.env`; no token value is recorded in any tracked file.
- **Collision check:** G-SOC is Eric's own feature. The guarded `llm/` directory (Torchiki's engine) is untouched. The owner approved the edits to the shared `server/src/config/env.js` and `server/src/server.js`. No other game, the catalog, the router or the shared `app.js` were edited.
- **Verification (15 October 2026, Eric's machine, working tree not committed):** `pnpm exec vitest run --project server --project client` (the `pnpm test:unit` script runs the same projects) finished with **417 passed, 21 failed across 438 tests in 37 files (7 files failed)**. The 21 failures are not caused by this feature: 2 are the known baseline composer failures in the G-SOC `components.test.js` ("emits the trimmed text on submit and clears the field", "sends on Enter but not on Shift+Enter"), and 19 are in files this feature does not touch (`ConnectionStatus.test.js`, `Town.test.js`, `threat-briefing/__tests__/audio.test.js`, `cli-cyber-defender/__tests__/components.test.js` and `demo.test.js`, `phishing-post-mortem/__tests__/components.test.js`). ## Feature organization

New work must be grouped by feature within the frontend and backend so teammates can present a clear end-to-end flow. Keep the established workspace boundaries; do not put server code or secrets in the client.

- Games use `client/src/games/<game-id>/` and `server/src/modules/games/<game-id>/`. Other new frontend features use `client/src/features/<feature-id>/` and their agreed `server/src/modules/<feature-id>/` module. Existing module identities take precedence: Cyber News Central uses `client/src/games/threat-briefing/` and the reserved `server/src/modules/news/`, with the existing `threat-briefing` catalog ID and route.
- Keep feature-only entry components, child components, composables, request wrappers, styles and assets together. Request wrappers reuse the shared Axios client. Group server routes, controllers, services, validation, provider adapters and feature-owned models within that feature module; create only layers the feature needs.
- Keep shared layout/navigation, authentication, HTTP handling and genuinely shared models/services in their existing shared locations. Register a feature through small explicit integration points; never duplicate the shell, auth, registry or results service to make a folder self-contained.
- Group tests by feature while respecting existing discovery: frontend `__tests__/` under the module, server `server/tests/unit/<feature-id>/` and `server/tests/integration/<feature-id>/`, browser `tests/e2e/<feature-id>.spec.js`. A different placement requires an approved matching test-configuration change.
- Each feature plan and final handover must list its client/server files, shared edits, tests and request/data flow. A short module README may explain this map for presentation. Do not create empty speculative layers or move unrelated existing features as part of this convention.

### Shared implementation blueprint

The following proposed persistence contract applies to the five scored games. Ranked play depends on the pending shared authentication, authorization and attempt/result foundation. Agree and implement that foundation as a separate task; individual games must not create competing versions.

**Module boundaries**

- Frontend: `client/src/games/<game-id>/`, Vue 3 `<script setup>`, plain JavaScript and scoped styles.
- Backend: `server/src/modules/games/<game-id>/`, containing routes, validators, scoring and private scenario data.
- Requests: reuse `client/src/services/api.js`; its base URL already contains `/api`.
- State: keep gameplay in a module composable using `ref`, `reactive` and `computed`. Reserve Pinia for shared account/context state.
- Visual boundary: follow [DESIGN.md](DESIGN.md#rules-for-new-pages-and-features). Game modules may choose their own design philosophy and scoped fonts/artwork/controls; shared navigation, town registration/cards and shared pages retain the exact shared pixel scheme. Verify accessibility/responsiveness and no style leakage.
- Town integration: all six activities have one catalog entry and building. SQL injection reuses its concrete route with saved practice; news, phishing, social-engineering, CLI and Clearance Tower (in the former regex shed) are playable behind their routes. A separately approved implementation must reuse its recorded ID, route and placement, then remove the preview flag only after verification. Do not add duplicate buildings or infer permission from a preview. Only genuinely new activities need an additional registration through DESIGN.md; no completion locks.

**Proposed shared API**

- `POST /api/games/:gameId/attempts` accepts `{ context: { kind: "personal" | "cohort", cohortId? } }`. The server validates membership, chooses the ruleset and creates the attempt.
- `GET /api/attempts/:attemptId` returns an owner-authorized public snapshot.
- `POST /api/attempts/:attemptId/actions` accepts `{ actionId, expectedRevision, type, payload }`.
- `POST /api/attempts/:attemptId/complete` accepts `{ actionId, expectedRevision }`; completion succeeds only when the game’s finish conditions are satisfied.
- Public snapshots contain `{ attemptId, gameId, rulesetVersion, status, revision, startedAt, deadlineAt, score, maxScore, publicState }`. Finished snapshots additionally contain `feedback` and `topicOutcomes`.

`actionId` is a client-generated UUID. Repeating the same action returns its recorded outcome; reusing its ID with different input fails. Apply revision checks and atomic updates so simultaneous requests cannot award duplicate points. Never accept player IDs, totals, achievements or authoritative timestamps from the browser.

**Proposed Mongoose model**

One shared `GameAttempt` base schema:

- `playerId`: required User ObjectId, derived from the session.
- `gameId`, `rulesetVersion`: server-selected, immutable strings.
- `context`: immutable `{ kind, cohortId }`; personal practice requires a null cohort.
- `status`: `active | completed | abandoned`.
- `startedAt`, `deadlineAt`, `completedAt`: server dates; deadline may be null.
- `revision`: nonnegative integer.
- `score`, `maxScore`: server-calculated numbers.
- `topicOutcomes`: `{ topic, achieved, feedbackCode }[]`.
- `processedActions`: bounded records of action ID, request hash and public outcome.
- `state`: a strict game-specific subdocument defined below, never an unrestricted `Mixed` object.

Index `{ playerId, gameId, startedAt }` and `{ "context.cohortId", gameId, status }`. Use explicit response serializers; never expose private answer keys or internal state wholesale. The completed attempt is the canonical result; downstream rewards must be idempotent.

All mutations require shared sessions, ownership checks, CSRF/origin protection and validated inputs. Until these dependencies exist, a prototype must clearly say that it is unsaved and cannot award persistent points.

**Shared acceptance**

Verify the core journey, invalid inputs, another user’s attempt ID, duplicate actions/completion and interrupted requests. Test responsive behavior at 375/576/768/992/1200/1440px, keyboard and touch access. Timed games need an untimed practice option excluded from rankings. Use only dedicated disposable `_test` databases.

### G-CLI — CLI Cyber Defender

**Concept:** Defend a fictional network through a simulated terminal during a 100-second incident shift.

**Difficulty:** ★★☆

**Required concepts:** IP addresses, firewall blocking, account security, incident containment and the distinction between an alert and a suitable response.

**Gameplay / interaction loop**

1. Read the command reference and start.
2. Receive eight incidents. Generate eight random integer windows of 10–15 seconds whose total is exactly 100 seconds.
3. Submit one defense command per incident, such as `block ip 192.0.2.10`, `disable user trainee-03` or `isolate host lab-02`.
4. A correct command before its deadline increases the streak. Award `100 × min(3, streak)` points.
5. A wrong command or missed window closes that incident and resets the streak. Maximum score: 2,100.
6. End with an incident-by-incident explanation of the appropriate defense.

All commands manipulate fictional game state. Never invoke a shell, firewall, operating-system account or real network target.

**Architectural blueprint**

- Route/ID: `/games/cli-cyber-defender` / `cli-cyber-defender`.
- Town entry: `cottage` at `{ x: 112, y: 112 }` in the shared catalog, reused by the implementation recorded below; no second building or route.
- `CliDefenderGame.vue`: owns the attempt and composes the following components.
- `TerminalPanel.vue`: props `{ lines, disabled }`; emits `submit-command(command)`.
- `IncidentBanner.vue`: props `{ incident, remainingMs }`.
- `ScoreHud.vue`: props `{ score, streak, multiplier, remainingMs }`.
- `CommandReference.vue`: displays syntax and touch-insertable command templates; emits `insert-template(template)`.
- `useCliDefender.js`: owns input, terminal history, pending request and the latest server snapshot. A 100ms `setInterval` refreshes displayed time from absolute server deadlines; it does not count ticks as elapsed time. Clear timers and abort requests on unmount.
- Shared action: `type: "command"`, payload `{ incidentId, command }`. Limit commands to 120 characters; normalize whitespace and command keywords, then parse only the documented grammar.
- Server selects incidents, timestamps receipt, verifies the current window and calculates streak/score. Client clock changes cannot extend play.
- Mongoose `state`: `{ mode, schedule: [{ incidentId, scenarioId, opensAt, closesAt, expectedAction, resolved, outcome }], streak, correctCount, missedCount }`. Keep `expectedAction` private.

**Acceptance:** Test exact deadline boundaries, incorrect targets, duplicate submissions, multiplier resets, background-tab throttling and timer cleanup. Mobile users can insert templates and enter targets without a desktop keyboard.

**Update (27 October 2026, SAVE-01):** runs now require sign-in and are owner-bound, and finished shifts are saved by the server through the shared attempts contract (server-recorded, max 2100). Timed runs save as standard and competitive and rank as "Timed runs" on class boards; practice saves as practice. The "nothing is persisted" and `saved: false` wording below is the original interim description. See the SAVE-01 record in TASKS.md.

**Implemented in this version (12 October 2026, Xin Lei):** the game is playable at `/games/cli-cyber-defender` and its catalog entry is no longer a preview. Because AUTH-01, AUTH-02, DATA-01 and GAME-01 do not exist yet, nothing is persisted: runs live in server memory for at most ten idle minutes, every response carries `saved: false`, and the player-facing copy says results are not saved yet. Seven interim feature-local endpoints stand in for the shared attempt contract: `POST /api/games/cli-cyber-defender/runs` with `{ mode: "timed" | "practice" }`, side-effect-free `GET .../runs/:runId`, `POST .../runs/:runId/commands` with `{ actionId, incidentId, command }`, `POST .../runs/:runId/hint` with `{ incidentId }` (practice only), `POST .../runs/:runId/pause` and `.../resume` (empty bodies) and `POST .../runs/:runId/complete` with `{ actionId }`; `server/src/modules/games/cli-cyber-defender/README.md` records the GAME-01 migration. Approved refinements to the loop above: timed windows are 10–15 seconds (100 in total; Xin Lei lengthened them from 5–10 / 60) and resolving a challenge opens the next one immediately; an unparseable command returns a hint and leaves the challenge open, while a parseable wrong action or target closes it; Practice (no timer) can be ended early and offers one hint per challenge; and each game draws three easy, three medium and two hard incidents in that order. Pausing, from the Menu or by hiding the tab, freezes the server clock, refuses commands with `CLI_RUN_PAUSED` and covers the full-size console until the player resumes. After each closed challenge the server returns the correct command, a one-line explanation and concept tags (attack type, confidentiality/integrity/availability at risk, containment stage); alert names, decoys included, are tappable; and the screen adds a Challenge n / 8 counter, score count-up, streak meter, outcome stamps, flash/shake, perfect-game confetti and optional generated sound, all reduced-motion aware or off by default. The briefing states the player's role (a security analyst on shift), shows a looping demo and confirms Start game in a "Ready to play?" dialog; during a game a Menu replaces Back to town (Sound, Pause, End practice, Leave game, which confirms that points won't be saved). Components beyond the list above: `BriefingPanel`, `DemoTerminal`, `GameMenu`, `ConfirmDialog`, `PausedPanel`, `ReviewNote`, `ConceptTags`, `CliIcon` and `ShiftDebrief` (summary tiles, topic table and collapsible incidents; Play again restarts the same mode and Main menu returns to the briefing). The module uses the DESIGN.md game exception through an original green/amber-on-black terminal set in Silkscreen and Azeret Mono, contained under `.cli-game`.

### G-SOC — LLM Social Engineering Simulator

**Concept:** A fictional corporate Slack-style conversation in which an LLM-controlled colleague uses urgency to request a synthetic password-reset code.

**Difficulty:** ★★☆

**Required concepts:** Social engineering, impersonation, urgency, reset-code confidentiality and verification through an independent channel.

**Gameplay / interaction loop**

1. Enter a clearly labeled simulation and receive a generated training code such as `TRAIN-482193`.
2. Exchange up to eight learner messages with the fictional colleague.
3. Choose whether to disclose the training code, verify the request through a simulated directory or report the conversation.
4. Sharing the training code ends the attempt with zero points.
5. Otherwise, completion awards 100 points for protecting the code, plus 25 each for independent verification and reporting.
6. Show a debrief explaining the pressure tactics and safe response. The model does not decide the score.

**Architectural blueprint**

- Route/ID: `/games/social-engineering-simulator` / `social-engineering-simulator`.
- Existing town entry: `office` at `{ x: 432, y: 520 }` in the shared catalog, now used by the playable, saved implementation documented below.
- `SocialEngineeringGame.vue`: owns the attempt.
- `ChatTimeline.vue`: props `{ messages, pending }`; accessible message log.
- `ChatComposer.vue`: props `{ disabled, maxLength }`; emits `send(text)`.
- `TrainingCodeCard.vue`: props `{ code }`; clearly identifies the code as fictional.
- `VerificationPanel.vue`: props `{ verified, disabled }`; emits `verify` and `report`.
- `useSocialEngineering.js`: owns draft text, transcript, pending/error state and retry action ID. Permit one pending message at a time.
- `POST /api/chat` accepts `{ attemptId, actionId, expectedRevision, message }`; message length 1–500 characters. Return `{ reply, attempt }`. Route through the shared attempt-validation/action service.
- Verification/reporting use shared actions `verify-colleague` and `report-conversation`, with empty payloads.
- Server builds conversation history from the stored attempt. Reject client-supplied system prompts, message roles, model names and provider URLs.
